The request still needs `test_copy: true`.\n108\tThe selected plugin's `change/references/hosted-test-lifecycle.md` provides exact\n109\treleased section selectors and pointers to individual action schemas.\n110\tFor a handoff file, `encode-file ABSOLUTE_FILE` returns its actual byte count,\n111\tSHA-256 and base64 content. It takes no output flag. Copy those returned values\n112\tunchanged; do not compute or guess a replacement digest.\n113\t\n114\tUse `observations --record ABSOLUTE_NEW_RECORD` before the final report. Read its\n115\tselected fields as needed. `observed_content_reads` contains successful visible\n116\tread metadata; `file_reads` retains Claude Read attempts, including failures.\n117\tUse the observed trace instead of rebuilding it with inventory lookups. Missing\n118\treads remain unknown; rereading now does not prove an earlier read. The capture\n119\tmay be incomplete, and subsequent report writes are not yet counted. Keep the\n120\trecord path as evidence instead of copying every command. This supplies no draft\n121\ttext, content judgement or lifecycle decision.\n122\t\n123\tThe returned `record` is the exact JSON **file**, even when it has no extension.\n124\tUse its `read_arguments` with the approved helper prefix. The index does not select a handoff base or certify a retry.\n132\t\n133\tRetain failed calls and recoveries. Do not claim an exhaustive failure count from memory.\n136\t"

Reuse a requirement (REQ) only if its meaning stays unchanged.                                              |\\n| `specification`       | `SPEC-`   | Defines the detailed behavior, interfaces, constraints, and rejection conditions.                                                                                            | Each requirement (REQ) selected for implementation needs specification (SPEC) coverage.                                                                  |\\n| `architecture`        | `ARCH-`   | Defines system structure and boundaries needed to meet significant requirements (REQ).                                                                                       | An active architecture (ARCH) directly addresses a requirement (REQ) selected by the work order (WO).                                                    |\\n| `adr`                 | `ADR-`    | Records an architecture decision (ADR), the alternatives, and their consequences.                                                                                            | An applicable architecture (ARCH) declares that a significant decision is required.                                                                      |\\n| `verification`        | `VER-`    | Defines how requirements (REQ) will be checked and what counts as a pass.                                                                                                    | Each selected requirement (REQ) needs verification coverage. They may support\\nor be referenced by formal artifacts but do not grant authority by themselves.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"47268c9ec1e3551fb547ad3d1ef7b8da0b773b52b3a4364f42c36cc64f98bf83\"}, {\"heading\": \"artifact-locations\", \"title\": \"Artifact locations\", \"anchor\": \"artifact-locations\", \"start_line\": 63, \"end_line\": 96, \"content\": \"## Artifact locations\\n\\nNew formal artifacts use these directories below\\n`docs/engineering/DOMAIN/`. `DOMAIN` is the selected domain name. These paths\\ndescribe storage, not authority; the artifact ID, metadata, and typed links\\nremain authoritative. Use the supported authoring command to obtain the actual\\npath. Specification (SPEC) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `specifies` | Each requirement (REQ) selected by a work order (WO) needs one or more selected active specifications (SPEC). |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", simplicity\", \"anchor\": \"design-simplicity\", \"start_line\": 21, \"end_line\": 58, \"content\": \"## Design simplicity\\n\\nFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\\nreports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\\nat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\\nor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\\nidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\\ndisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\\nfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\\nComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\\nrequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\\nboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\\nimplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\\nusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\\ncan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\\ncan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\\nor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\\nor dependencies also has a cost.\\n\\nApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\\nand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\\nfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\\nin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\\nis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\\nsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\\narchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\\n\\nThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\\nmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"29e9b6296f39215c4b2ad36659c0674f0b20650682db9aa1187fc97871cfdb06\"}, {\"heading\": \"intent\", \"title\": \"intent\", \"anchor\": \"intent\", \"start_line\": 76, \"end_line\": 84, \"content\": \"## intent\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Explain the present problem, who it affects, and the outcome wanted.\\n- What is the smallest useful outcome, and which needs and constraints are actually agreed?\\n- Say how the owner will know the change helped; an honest baseline may be `not measured`.\\n- State the important scope limits. Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"291e8274f0c400a3d2d1ecdd56f2c10d68ab6cb40f667076a034e44d87c05e42\"}, {\"heading\": \"capability\", \"title\": \"capability\", \"anchor\": \"capability\", \"start_line\": 85, \"end_line\": 93, \"content\": \"## capability\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Say what an actor should be able to do and any relevant conditions.\\n- Does each supported case serve an agreed need under those conditions?\\n- Link the intent it serves. The graph lists the derived requirements.\\n- Put implementation details where they help the implementer; no required `can ... under` wording.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"ee6452eeeb618cbe10c941638e7f6be38d2709f53e169920c2d3d012c94ce978\"}, {\"heading\": \"requirement\", \"title\": \"requirement\", \"anchor\": \"requirement\", \"start_line\": 59, \"end_line\": 75, \"content\": \"## requirement\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- State the observable behavior and why it is needed. Ordinary language is accepted; `SHALL` is optional.\\n- What agreed outcome does this obligation serve, for whom and under which conditions? Separate requirements from assumptions and speculative needs.\\n- Include a concrete acceptance condition in `Examples`, `Acceptance`, `measure`, `verification_notes`, or a linked verification contract.\\n- Name the source capability and a verification method. Supported method arrays contain `test`, `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration`; retained text methods remain readable.\\n- Replace unfinished placeholders and resolve blocking decisions before approval.\\n\\n### Guidance\\n\\nWrite enough for someone to implement and check the behavior. Use the template's\\nsections when helpful. Word counts, sentence shapes and numbers of code references\\nare not checked. Writing hints never block approval. Optional metadata must still\\nhave a valid value when supplied.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"333872b200842af881c6c22cfcb0631897071421f423885b2702ac6b72c75770\"}, {\"heading\": \"specification\", \"title\": \"specification\", \"anchor\": \"specification\", \"start_line\": 94, \"end_line\": 107, \"content\": \"## specification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Explain what an implementation must do, including meaningful failure behavior.\\n- Is each promised behavior necessary? Could a simpler contract meet the agreed need? Prescribe a mechanism only when it is a necessary constraint.\\n- Link the requirements and show an example someone can check.\\n- Give a rule a stable identifier when evidence or a decision needs to refer to it.\\n- Check that supplied rule references exist and identifiers are unambiguous.\\n\\nMissing summaries and ambiguous rule references may produce draft hints. Hints\\nhelp a reviewer; they do not withhold approval. Existing approved text need not\\nbe rewritten to adopt these checklists.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"7aeefa5f4ed363ecdef5185d2b776c6fdd9146ece8e2bfd7ae1ee852f0acf6e1\"}, {\"heading\": \"verification\", \"title\": \"verification\", \"anchor\": \"verification\", \"start_line\": 126, \"end_line\": 135, \"content\": \"## verification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\\n- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\\n- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\\n- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Cover distinct relevant outcomes, reuse applicable evidence, and avoid mirroring every implementation branch or combination.\\n- Pass criteria name the platforms and the evaluator.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e6d36e5fe7a23845861e67079317f856b4f892d7f17c4f3d4dbdb4476edb7720\"}, {\"heading\": \"work_order\", \"title\": \"work_order\", \"anchor\": \"work_order\", \"start_line\": 136, \"end_line\": 146, \"content\": \"## work_order\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- `[execution_scope].paths` is exact: files or component-prefix directories, nothing wider.\\n- `[assurance]` is classified and its rationale names what later decisions depend on.\\n- In scope and out of scope are both stated; the decision envelope says what the implementer may and may not decide.\\n- Stop conditions and the completion report format are stated.\\n- Is every planned change needed for the agreed outcome? Include removal or adjustment of obsolete behavior, tests and instructions within the approved scope.\\n- Inspect supporting paths before approval and assess the supplied plan through [Draft work orders](harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Keep reasons in Expected change surface; do not add a separate planning artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e8d3be9bfae6d0679a08544ac587fa73e1a26e026d23373c29e1927e0803e7b8\"}]}]}",

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"c4ffde0efff3a57df41cf6b282896e6827ea289607f8b73faa8f7c09d0c70213\", \"bytes\": 8654, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nDefinitions, work scope and verification requirements are ready for work planning.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"3977586afdaba8e900cfd38bfd72b02ccf3d011048dae4b247fe81a24c64c9cc\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#links-from-work-orders-wo](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#links-from-work-orders-wo), [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope) and [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Apply [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#design-simplicity) and [../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#work_order](../ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md#work_order).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"d2a9a7707114a465ebde20884e2a996807663ab4aa94ff11008f2360617efe50\"}, {\"heading\": \"procedure\", \"title\": \"Procedure\", \"anchor\": \"procedure\", \"start_line\": 11, \"end_line\": 143, \"content\": \"## Procedure\\n\\n### Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements\\n\\n**Inputs:** The outcome, scope, artifact inventory, definition drafts,\\namendment proposals, verification contracts (VER), risks (RISK), and\\nunresolved decisions (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** New or completed work orders (WO) in `draft`.\\n  Applicable risks (RISK) and open decisions (DEC) updated with the actual\\n  work order (WO) links. Lifecycle states and dispositions are preserved.\\n  Approved work orders (WO) remain unchanged; proposed scope changes require\\n  review.\\n- **Transient working material:** The review handoff and evaluator results.\\n  Retain these under the same conditions as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the implementation and follow its callers, references and consumers.\\n   Check tests and fixtures, instructions and documentation, packaging and CI,\\n   and verification outputs where they apply. Keep the working inventory in\\n   the conversation or temporary storage outside the repository.\\n   Group each complete, independently verifiable outcome into one bounded\\n   work order with its necessary supporting changes.\\n2. Select an existing work order (WO) in `draft` for a unit only if its scope\\n   matches. Otherwise, create a new work order (WO) with the commands below.\\n3. Complete each draft using the field table below. In Expected change\\n   surface, name the inspected paths and give a short reason for each.\\n   Explain non-applicable areas and unknown destinations briefly.\\n4. Check that every selected requirement (REQ) has the specification (SPEC)\\n   and verification contract (VER) coverage defined in [Complete work scope](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#complete-work-scope).\\n5. Apply `## work_order` \\u2192 `### Checklist` in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n6. Add the new work order (WO) IDs to applicable `threatens` relations in risks\\n   (RISK) and `concerns` or `blocks` relations in open decisions (DEC). Keep\\n   every blocked ID in `concerns`. Preserve any required pairing between the\\n   risk (RISK) and decision (DEC).\\n7. If the planned change needs a missing release or operating contract,\\n   use [Prepare a release or operating contract](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#prepare-a-release-or-operating-contract) now that the work\\n   order IDs exist. Include its draft in the proposed package.\\n8. Run the validation command below.\\n9. Correct findings within the proposed drafts. Rerun validation after edits.\\n   Report unrelated findings separately. Retain findings that need a decision\\n   or an accepted-definition amendment as unresolved dependencies.\\n10. Run the selected-work check below for each proposed work order (WO).\\n    Supply every planned file from Expected change surface with\\n    `--planned-path`. Inspect `scope.preparation`: resolve uncovered or\\n    invalid paths in the draft before requesting approval. A successful\\n    command exit does not establish coverage or a complete impact analysis.\\n    Explain future generated destinations using\\n    [Generated outputs](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#generated-outputs).\\n11. Present the outcome, scope, artifact IDs and paths, validation results,\\n    unresolved decisions (DEC), and unapplied amendments. Include the\\n    evaluator's reported next action for each selected work order (WO).\\n\\n| Field or section | Content |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| `Objective` | The outcome this work order (WO) will deliver. |\\n| `In scope` / `Out of scope` | The boundaries from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) assigned to this work order (WO). |\\n| `Constraints` | The conditions from [Define the limits of the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#define-the-limits-of-the-change) that apply to this work. |\\n| `Expected change surface` | Inspected planned paths, the reason for each, and unresolved destinations. |\\n| `[execution_scope].paths` | Exact repository-relative files or component directories ending in `/`. Use forward slashes; no wildcards, absolute paths, or `..`. |\\n| `Authorized decision envelope` | What the implementer may decide and what requires another accountable decision. |\\n| `Required verification` / `Evidence to record` | The checks and retained outputs defined in [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified). |\\n| `Stop and escalate conditions` | The conditions that require pausing this work. |\\n| `Completion report format` | The required report of completed behavior, checks, limitations, and next accountable decision. |\\n| `[assurance]` | `commit_bound_verification`, its `rationale`, and `decided_by`. Apply the classification rules in [Assurance classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). Record the human who confirmed the classification; an agent may propose it and record that human's decision. |\\n| `[relations]` | `implements` \\u2192 requirements (REQ); `specifications` \\u2192 specifications (SPEC); `verification` \\u2192 verification contracts (VER); `architecture` \\u2192 applicable architectures (ARCH) and required architecture decisions (ADR). |\\n\\nFor PR-based work, include the proposed [review-publication grant](AUTHORITY.md#review-publication-authority)\\nin the decision envelope. Name its repository, source branch, target branch\\nand selected work. Include both the ready-record publication and the later\\ndecision-only push in the expected scope. Present that grant explicitly with\\nimplementation approval; do not infer it from ordinary work authority.\\n\\nIf the assurance classification has not been confirmed, present it as a\\nproposal in the review handoff. Report the missing decision. Do not fill\\n`decided_by` with the draft author's name or an example identity to satisfy\\nvalidation. The human may confirm the classification while reviewing the\\nwork order; record that decision before previewing approval.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor each new work order (WO), run the creation sequence from [Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions).\\nInspect the preview before creation; replace `WO-ID` with its `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type work_order --id WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nValidate the formal artifacts after completing the drafts and links:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\n```\\n\\nInspect each proposed work order (WO) using its actual ID:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nTo assess the proposed file list, repeat `--planned-path` for every file:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --planned-path PATH --planned-path ANOTHER-PATH --json\\n```\\n\\nThis option is available only when the selected released evaluator supports\\nit. If it is unavailable, report that version gap; do not run candidate\\nsource to govern the repository.\\n\\nThe optional planning view evaluates no checkpoint. It reports explicit\\nscope matches, existing automatic admission, uncovered paths and invalid\\ndeclarations. It does not inspect dependencies for the agent, prove that\\nthe supplied list is complete, or turn planned paths into observed changes.\\nDo not combine it with checkpoint or actual-change inputs.\\n\\nValidation does not approve the change. The checkpoint-free `check` reports\\nlifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint gates.\\n\\n**Completion:** Draft work orders (WO) are presented for review with their\\ngoverning artifacts and evaluator results. Every unresolved dependency is\\nidentified. Preparation does not approve or start execution.\\n\\n**Later use:** The authorization process reviews this artifact package,\\nresolves approval blockers, and applies permitted lifecycle transitions.\\nOnly the formal artifacts carry lasting definitions and work authority;\\ntransient working material is not persisted in the repository.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"cfcd1bbe3d8874c6ed9fdd4f56f01ef65c16562f552fcbf390b7a0a82dd519df\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false

## Event 57: user tool_result

{
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_013KtyBh6ed97CgfPv9e9BSM",
  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"892ec2fedefc272f6bc2580268395e487f392cf5d92a3ea81fac015761bc87a8\", \"bytes\": 8641, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"links-from-work-orders-wo\", \"title\": \"Links from work orders (WO)\", \"anchor\": \"links-from-work-orders-wo\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 28, \"content\": \"## Links from work orders (WO)\\n\\nA work order (WO) selects the definitions and verification contracts (VER) that govern\\nits execution. Links to requirements (REQ) alone are not enough.\\n\\n| Source \\u2192 target | Relation | Meaning and coverage |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Requirement (REQ) | `implements` | Names every requirement (REQ) in the authorized work scope. |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Specification (SPEC) | `specifications` | Names the specifications (SPEC) governing those requirements (REQ). |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Architecture (ARCH) or Architecture decision (ADR) | `architecture` | Names every applicable architecture (ARCH) and every required architecture decision (ADR). |\\n| Work order (WO) \\u2192 Verification contract (VER) | `verification` | Names the verification contracts (VER) for the work. |\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) can declare these links in its metadata. The IDs\\nbelow are examples, not records created by this document.\\n\\n```toml\\n[relations]\\nimplements = [\\\"REQ-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\nspecifications = [\\\"SPEC-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\nverification = [\\\"VER-EXAMPLE-001\\\"]\\n```\\n\\nThe referenced requirement (REQ) must also link back to a capability (CAP) and intent (INT).\\nAdd architecture (ARCH) links when architecture (ARCH) applies; do not invent them just to\\nfill a field.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e5892787048f79f437b0395f9e1269152ce3d1d1b6fb28994f42849bd12a4f54\"}, {\"heading\": \"complete-work-scope\", \"title\": \"Complete work scope\", \"anchor\": \"complete-work-scope\", \"start_line\": 47, \"end_line\": 65, \"content\": \"## Complete work scope\\n\\nEvery requirement (REQ) selected by a work order (WO)\\nMUST link back to active capabilities (CAP) and intents (INT). It MUST also\\nhave selected active specifications (SPEC) and verification contracts (VER).\\nReuse existing artifacts where their scope applies.\\n\\nA work order should cover one complete bounded outcome, including its\\nforeseeable tests, documentation and retained evidence. Use exact file paths\\nby default. Use a narrow existing component directory when the affected\\nfiles belong to that component. A matching directory does not authorize\\nunrelated behavior.\\n\\nPrepare this scope through\\n[Draft work orders](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements).\\nDo not split predictable supporting changes into extra work orders merely\\nbecause they use different file types. A genuine expansion after approval\\nstill needs the applicable human decision; preserve the approved history.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"021ca3cdecffebe415ce9df445c0c48e24a0acde7609d25220c0fb7d470e054e\"}, {\"heading\": \"assurance-classification\", \"title\": \"Assurance classification\", \"anchor\": \"assurance-classification\", \"start_line\": 86, \"end_line\": 116, \"content\": \"## Assurance classification\\n\\nEach work order (WO) in `approved` or\\n`in_progress` MUST set `assurance.commit_bound_verification` to `required`\\nor `not_required`.\\n\\nUse `required` when later decisions depend on the correctness of changes to\\ncode, required checks, CI configuration, harness policy, artifact definitions,\\nor their links. These are examples of \\u201cchanged trusted state\\u201d: information or\\nbehavior that others will rely on when accepting, releasing, or operating the\\nresult.\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) changes the code that validates artifact links.\\nA later release decision relies on that validation being correct. The work\\norder (WO) therefore needs verification tied to its exact candidate commit.\\n\\nUse `not_required` only when the work solely records or transports a\\nverification, release, supersession, publication, or deployment decision\\nthat an authorized human has already made. It does not make a new decision\\nor change the definitions, implementation, or evidence behind that decision.\\n\\nFor example, a work order (WO) commits an already authorized release decision\\nin a release record (RLS), with no change to the released code, scope, or\\nsupporting evidence. The work records the decision; it does not reassess the\\nrelease.\\n\\nA work order (WO) that combines both kinds of work MUST be split or classified\\n`required`. `not_required` removes the obligation to prepare a new\\nverification record (VREC) for that work; it does not remove required checks\\nor the links to verification contracts (VER).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ad947ba93cfc84640c82a0b165448ce2c15f04beed36f65a3dc031e4287aab3\"}, {\"heading\": \"generated-outputs\", \"title\": \"Generated outputs\", \"anchor\": \"generated-outputs\", \"start_line\": 66, \"end_line\": 85, \"content\": \"## Generated outputs\\n\\nExisting scope rules admit the selected work order's own file, records that\\ndirectly verify or release that work order, and those records' declared\\nevaluator-evidence paths. This does not admit those records' parent directories.\\nThe selected work order's own packet directory is also already admitted by\\nthe existing scope rules; the planning report identifies that separate rule.\\n\\nList other planned evidence, review and log destinations explicitly in the\\nwork order's scope unless an existing rule covers them. A neighboring file\\nor a record linked only to another work order is not automatically admitted.\\nThe conditional rule for recording risks during execution remains unchanged;\\nit is not a blanket admission of future files during preparation.\\n\\nIf a future record's ID is not allocated, mark its destination as unresolved\\nand explain the relationship rule expected to cover it. Do not create a\\nrecord or invent an ID to complete the plan. Once the evaluator prepares\\nthe record, assess its actual returned record and evidence paths. An\\nunresolved destination is not an already covered file.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"21cb2760e5cf61158c5d19cca2af8dbe727ecf3df32247987cc1636059bde3e9\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

It is not copied from candidate output.\n\n## Requirement-to-evidence matrix\n\n| Requirement | Method | Case/evidence | Pass condition |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| REQ-P3-001 | test | C1 exact-greeting assertion; `docs/engineering/lifecycle-pilot/evidence/WO-P3-001/greeting-assertion.json` | `type(greeting()) is str` and `greeting() == 'Hello rehearsal'` are both true at the candidate commit; the command exits 0 |\n\n## Acceptance scenarios\n\n**C1 (planned, not yet executed).** Covers P3-GREET-001 and P3-GREET-002.\n\n- Input: `src/greeting.py` at the exact candidate commit, without local changes.\n- Platform: the selected hosted test-copy runtime, `linux/amd64` with Python `3.13.16`.\n- Evaluator: released SE Harness evaluator `0.22.1`\n  (archive `cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053`)\n  captures the candidate commit.\n- Command, from the repository root:\n\n```text\npython -c \"import sys; sys.path.insert(0, 'src'); from greeting import greeting; r = greeting(); assert type(r) is str and r == 'Hello rehearsal', repr(r); print(repr(r))\"\n```\n\n- Pass: exit status 0 and output `'Hello rehearsal'`. Exact `==` comparison\n  with a `str` also fails on any extra whitespace or newline (P3-GREET-002).\n- Fail: non-zero exit; the assertion message shows the actual `repr`.\n\n## Evidence retention\n\nRetain the command, platform, Python version, candidate commit, exit status\nand output in\n`docs/engineering/lifecycle-pilot/evidence/WO-P3-001/greeting-assertion.json`.\n\n## Residual uncertainty\n\n- The check covers one call on one platform. `greeting()` has no inputs, so no\n  other cases are defined.\n"

Use `not_required` only when the work solely records or transports an already authorized verification, release, supersession, publication, or deployment decision.   "content": "1\t# Hosted artifact graph: sandbox protocol and implementation\n2\t\n3\tThis guide explains the approved SPEC-HAG-001/002/003 protocol. The private\n4\tservice and candidate remote client are implemented under WO-HAG-001. Development\n5\tqualification is retained in VREC-HAG-004. Use [the operating procedure](../../server/README.md) to build and test\n6\tthe exact combination. Git remains authoritative. This is not a public service.\n7\t\n8\tDEC-HAG-001 is decided. The bounded revision in DEC-HAG-002 selects public\n9\tevaluator 0.22.1 for this sandbox; candidate client 0.22.2 is separate.\n10\t\n11\tPhase 3 adds an explicit private test-copy lifecycle pilot under WO-HAG-008 and\n12\tSPEC-HAG-007. Git remains authoritative. The pilot uses separately versioned\n13\t[closed operations](../../server/contracts/lifecycle-v2.md), exact multi-file\n14\tsnapshots and ordinary disposable Git history. Its eight required VER-HAG-006\n15\tscenarios passed, and mmzen verified\n16\t[VREC-HAG-005](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/verification-records/VREC-HAG-005.md).\n17\tPR #542 integrated that result. The separate native Codex/Claude qualification is\n18\t[in progress under WO-HAG-009](../engineering/hosted-artifact-graph/evidence/WO-HAG-009/report.md).\n19\tAuthentication and database ACL implementation remain deferred. The existing\n20\tprivate access controls and RISK-HAG-001 remain in place.\n21\t\n22\t## Project and source identities\n23\t\n24\tThe explicit operator initialization command allocates a UUIDv4 `project_id`\n25\tonce and stores it on Project with `command_version = 0` and `schema_revision = 1`.\n26\tAn operator may supply a fixed UUID for a reproducible disposable test. Repeating\n27\tinitialization confirms the same identity; it cannot silently replace a store.\n28\tThe readable name `hosted-artifact-poc` is a display name, outside revision hashes.\n29\tClients obtain the UUID from initialization output or authenticated status.\n30\tRequests never allocate projects and never take a principal identity on trust.\n31\t\n32\tThe reference source is `https://github.com/mmzen/se_harness`, Git SHA-1 commit\n33\t`82f5a0ed7438a73da8f03aee23ad02a9b8cc09e1`. The source manifest lists every formal\n34\tartifact path, ID, blob OID, byte length and raw SHA-256. Generate it from Git\n35\tblobs using the released discovery list, not Explorer output or a worktree read.\n36\tOn Windows, `git archive` needs `-c core.autocrlf=false`: archive defaults can\n37\tconvert LF to CRLF even when the blob is LF. Compare each exported artifact with\n38\t`git cat-file` bytes. Preserve original bytes, including CRLF when present in Git.\n39\t\n40\tThe operator mounts the pinned source/evidence snapshot read-only. Import requests\n41\tidentify this configured source; they cannot make the server fetch URLs or read\n42\tarbitrary host paths. Check every manifest entry against its mounted bytes, then\n43\tuse the released parser for the canonical artifact set. Missing, duplicate,\n44\textra or conflicting entries prevent complete import. Supporting code/evidence\n45\tbelongs to the immutable historical resolver, outside the 64 MiB artifact limit.\n46\t\n47\t## Revision and baseline bytes\n48\t\n49\t`server/contracts/remote-v1.json` names the exact fields. A StoredRevision contains\n50\t`revision_id`, `envelope` and `document_base64`. The envelope binds the full raw\n51\tdocument SHA-256; the store verifies it against decoded bytes. Imported provenance\n52\tcontains the original repository/object format/commit and Git blob OID. Draft\n53\tprovenance records the authenticated principal, operation key and server-assigned\n54\tcreation time once. An accepted retry returns the retained bytes and time.\n55\t\n56\tCanonical JSON uses UTF-8 without BOM, sorted object keys, compact separators,\n57\tliteral Unicode, no Unicode normalization and no floats. Reject duplicate object\n58\tkeys and non-finite numbers before decoding into a map. Semantic sets are sorted\n59\tand deduplicated before encoding: relation targets, artifact selection keys and\n60\tresolved relation triples. Preserve meaningful sequence order. Raw document\n61\tbytes receive no line-ending or Unicode conversion for the new revision scheme.\n62\t\n63\tThe revision hash input is `se-harness-artifact-revision/v1`, LF, then canonical\n64\tenvelope bytes. `revision_id` is `sha256:` plus the lowercase hex digest. The\n65\tbaseline hash input uses `se-harness-artifact-baseline/v1`, LF and the canonical\n66\tmanifest; its identifier is that scheme plus `:sha256:` and the hex digest.\n67\tNeither hash payload includes its own derived ID or a future verification record.\n68\tThe baseline manifest binds sorted selection, resolved revision-to-revision\n69\ttriples, provenance and the exact evaluator identity. A missing relation target\n70\tprevents a complete baseline. Saved incomplete drafts remain distinct from\n71\tcomplete frozen selections and from approved definitions.\n72\t\n73\tThe fixture file `canonical-v1.json` supplies exact JSON strings and hash-input\n74\tbytes for three connected draft artifacts, LF/CRLF, composed/decomposed Unicode,\n75\ta body edit, sequence order and a baseline. These are synthetic byte examples,\n76\tnot claimed receipts. Its all-zero parent baseline is an explicit fixture marker.\n77\tFuture serializer tests must consume these fixed expectations; do not regenerate\n78\texpected hashes from serializer output.\n79\t\n80\t## Command-specific fields\n81\t\n82\tEvery mutation carries the project ID, operation key, expected project version,\n83\texact evaluator archive/payload identity, and candidate client version/wheel hash.\n84\tThe server checks compatibility against the qualified tuple before mutation.\n85\tThe authenticated principal comes from credentials; claimed component identity\n86\tdoes not grant authority. Reject unknown fields and contradictory selectors.\n87\t\n88\t| Operation | Required selection and content | Accepted effect |\n89\t| --- | --- | --- |\n90\t| `import` | Complete configured source manifest; no baseline/context selector | Original immutable revisions and imported baseline, deterministic report, receipt, one project-version increment |\n91\t| `draft-open` | Base baseline ID and selected work-order ID | New UUID context at version 0; unchanged base; receipt and project-version increment |\n92\t| `create-artifact` | Context ID/version, domain, supported type; ID or explicit null for allocation | New draft revision and proposed selection; context and project versions increment once |\n93\t| `revise-artifact` | Context ID/version, artifact ID, expected selected revision, complete base64 document | New draft revision and derived relations; replace only that context selection; increment both versions |\n94\t| `freeze` | Context ID/version | New immutable baseline from the resolved complete view; project version increments; context stays open with its original base and version |\n95\t\n96\tAn empty project has no baseline to select. Import therefore has no view selector.\n97\tOperation lookup is `GET /v1/projects/{project}/operations/{key}` and is scoped to\n98\tthe authenticated principal. It requires neither a baseline nor a current version.\n99\tReadiness also has no view selector. These are the applicable-field interpretations\n100\tof HAG-API-001/002, not changes to the named protocol schemes.\n101\t\n102\tThe draft view is its base plus explicit proposals. New IDs are reserved across\n103\tall retained project artifacts, not just the selected baseline. The command adapter\n104\tuses the released authoring template and allocation behavior in a disposable\n105\tprojection. The isolated public 0.22.1 evaluator supplies standalone draft\n106\tadmission. Protected lifecycle/provenance/evidence fields remain protected.\n107\t\n108\tFreeze does not rebase, close, clear or otherwise change its context. Repeating an already accepted\n111\toperation key returns its original receipt even if live versions have advanced.\n112\tA different request under the same principal/project/key returns conflict.\n113\t\n114\t## Atomic acceptance and reads\n115\t\n116\tBind evaluation to project version N and the exact context/revision selection.\n117\tIn a short explicit database transaction, conditionally change the same Project\n118\tguard from N to N+1 and recheck context/revision guards. Commit the new content,\n119\tderived edges, selection changes and accepted receipt in that transaction. A\n120\tcompeting guard update or constraint conflict refuses; a process mutex is not\n121\tthe transaction boundary. Do not retry against newer input without caller action.\n122\t\n123\tImported content and repeated source identity cannot disagree. {
  "tool_use_id": "toolu_01W15kaW4ppqA5oKVtRzbySP",
  "type": "tool_result",
  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORIZE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"991b526d6280554dcca6901c1790e5519b8894e08635a1f10618aa84a29aaf28\", \"bytes\": 15212, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe proposed package needs validation, review or a human authorization decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8b18d925f39f314acc9ea6bcc9820015c4c9430d836144e85bc31b670752371b\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nFor reviewing each definition, use its selected [Type checklist](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#type-checklists). Before review, read [AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights](AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights) and [AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation](AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation). Before approving work, read [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). For an open or deferred decision, read [RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"9aa32693606102a5aa74b2e4a614aed6904471e5f618dea93356baac97c86ca2\"}, {\"heading\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"title\": \"Assess the proposed artifact package\", \"anchor\": \"assess-the-proposed-artifact-package\", \"start_line\": 32, \"end_line\": 83, \"content\": \"### Assess the proposed artifact package\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) IDs, proposed definitions, validation\\nresults, and existing decision records (DEC).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None created or modified.\\n- **Transient working material:** A review list identifying the selected\\n  artifacts, their states, applicable checks, and reported blockers.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run validation for the repository.\\n2. Inspect each selected work order (WO) with the command below.\\n3. Read the work order (WO) and its returned reading manifest.\\n4. Review the governing definitions against the relevant checklists in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n5. Check that the proposed work has defined scope, verification coverage,\\n   assurance classification, and accountable humans for the required decisions.\\n6. List the blockers affecting the selected package. Keep unrelated findings\\n   separate.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl validate REPO --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint transition --target approved --json\\n```\\n\\nRead the result of each required check. A `pass` satisfies that check only;\\nit does not approve the artifacts or authorize implementation. If a required\\ncheck returns `fail` or `not_assessable`, report its identifier and stated\\nreason, then follow \\u201cIf a procedure is blocked.\\u201d Do not proceed with the\\naffected approval.\\n\\nThe last command evaluates work-order approval gates. A checkpoint-free\\n`check` only reports lifecycle context. Definition artifacts cannot be selected\\nwith `check`; their transition gates are evaluated by the previews in [Apply the selected transitions](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#apply-the-selected-transitions).\\n\\nBefore proposing a definition for approval, resolve its template placeholders.\\nIf it contains an `Open decisions` section, confirm that no unresolved decision\\nremains before setting that section to `None`. Do not remove an unresolved\\nquestion merely to make validation pass.\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected package has an explicit list of review findings\\nand approval blockers. No approval is inferred from these checks.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Resolve approval blockers](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#resolve-approval-blockers) resolves the blockers. [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision) presents the package for a\\nhuman decision.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2ea6b051236e13dd427317722d16ce7941ef5a4eb4789f7fdf568ab3d0cae3c0\"}, {\"heading\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"title\": \"Apply the selected transitions\", \"anchor\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"start_line\": 221, \"end_line\": 270, \"content\": \"### Apply the selected transitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact artifact IDs, target states, accountable human identities,\\nreviewed file digests, decisions from [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision), and clarification answers from\\n[Define the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#procedure) that must be retained in the transition reason.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only explicitly selected definitions and work orders\\n  (WO) receive the applied state and lifecycle-history entries.\\n- **Transient working material:** The preview and apply results, including\\n  any refused transition.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"decision-rights\", \"title\": \"Decision rights\", \"anchor\": \"decision-rights\", \"start_line\": 19, \"end_line\": 80, \"content\": \"## Decision rights\\n\\nA profile describes who performs an action. A decision right describes which\\naction that person or agent is authorized to perform. Being a Human does not\\ngrant every right in this table. Being an Agent grants none by itself.\\n\\nUse the artifact's recorded ownership and any existing explicit delegation to\\nidentify the accountable human. Resolve a team name or ownership label to the\\nhuman making this decision. A proposed `owners` value, an example actor name,\\nor authorship of a draft MUST NOT assign decision authority.\\n\\nApproval, verification acceptance, and release decisions MUST be made by an\\nauthorized human. An agent MAY prepare the material and apply that human's\\nrecorded decision when authorized to do so. Applying the decision does not\\nmake the agent its author. The selected IDs, reviewed content, target states,\\nand reason MUST still match the decision at apply time.\\n\\nThe following stable IDs name rights and operations, not additional profiles.\\nAll applicable gates remain required.\\n\\nThese DR-* identifiers come from the selected evaluator's decision-right\\ncatalogue. Use the right and exact action returned by that evaluator. Legacy\\nowner labels identify recorded accountabilities; they do not create additional\\nHuman/Agent profiles or assign a decision to a person by themselves.\\n\\n| Right ID                   | Accountable profile and authority                                                                                                  | Required input                                                                                                    | Result                                                                                               |\\n|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `DR-DEFINITION-DECIDE`     | Human authorized to decide the selected definition.                                                                                | Complete definition, exact proposed state, and required checks.                                                   | Decision on that definition only.                                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-SELECT`             | Human authorized to approve the work scope.                                                                                        | Complete governing chain, bounded work order, and assurance classification.                                       | Approval or rejection of the selected work order.                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-START`              | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Unchanged approved scope and passing start checks.                                                                | The selected work order becomes `in_progress`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-WO-COMPLETE`           | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Completed scope, passing handoff checks, and retained evidence.                                                   | The selected work order becomes `implemented`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-VREC-PREPARE`          | Human or Agent under the approval of every selected work order.                                                                    | Exact candidate, selected work and verification contracts, retained evidence, and passing preparation checks.     | One `ready` verification record; no acceptance decision.                                             |\\n| `DR-VREC-DECIDE`           | Human authorized to accept verification for the selected candidate.                                                                | Ready record, assessed evidence, and the exact verify, reject, or supersede decision.                             | Decision on that verification record only.                                                           |\\n| `DR-DELIVERY-SELECT`       | Human authorized for the chosen integration or release-preparation path.                                                           | Exact candidate, destination, and coverage required by that path.                                                 | One path-specific instruction.                                                                       |\\n| `DR-RLS-PREPARE`           | Human authorizes the release inputs; a Human or Agent may carry out the preparation.                                               | Exact release contract, candidate, included work, verification coverage, version, and passing preparation checks. | One `ready` release record; no release decision.                                                     |\\n| `DR-RLS-DECIDE`            | Human authorized to make the release decision.                                                                                     | Ready release record, assessed contract obligations, and the exact release or reject decision.                    | Decision on that release record only.                                                                |\\n| `DR-EXTERNAL-ACTION`       | Human authorized for the exact external action. A Human or Agent may execute the authorized operation.                             | Action, immutable target, destination, prerequisites, and recovery conditions.                                    | Authorization limited to that operation; the subsequent tool result records its actual effects.      |\\n| `DR-RELATED-RECORD-SELECT` | Human or Agent; no decision authority is needed for inspection.                                                                    | Exact existing artifact ID.                                                                                       | Read-only selection.                                                                                 |\\n| `DR-REMEDIATION-SCOPE`     | Human authorized to change the affected definition or work scope.                                                                  | Failed criterion and proposed bounded correction.                                                                 | Explicit authority for the revised scope.                                                            |\\n| `DR-DECISION-DISPOSE`      | Human accountable for the artifact blocked by the decision; for a deviation, the human accountable for the affected specification. | Open or deferred decision, selected option or disposition, exact reason, and any required revisit trigger.        | Recorded disposition and any declared paired-risk effect. Other blocked artifacts keep their states. |\\n\\nFor a lifecycle decision, the `--decision` value identifies its actual\\ndecision-maker. When an agent applies a human decision, that value is the\\nhuman who made it. For start and completion under approved execution, it is\\nthe actual Human or Agent executor. Supplying either identity does not grant\\nauthority. Preparation `--owner` values identify the preparation actor, not\\na future assurance or release decision-maker.\\n\\nFor `decide`, when an owner label differs from the actual human's identity,\\nuse `--authority-owner OWNER` only after establishing that human's authority\\nfor the exact decision. Keep `--decision ACTOR` as the actual human. The\\nevaluator checks that OWNER holds the existing decision right and records it\\nseparately as `authority_owner`. With the option absent, ACTOR must itself\\nmatch an eligible owner. The command does not authenticate either claim or\\ngrant consent. Do not infer a binding from the decision's own owners, an\\nearlier approval, Git configuration or the agent's identity.\\n\\nSilence, elapsed time, a commit, a pull request, tool execution, or a passing\\ncheck MUST NOT count as a human decision. A rejection MUST include a non-empty\\nreason. Supersession MUST name exactly one eligible successor. The decision\\nand its resulting state MUST remain specific to the selected artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ecaeb29429bcaa4eac99857f2f921e270c4865d1edeec96f536d09eb6c3130a\"}, {\"heading\": \"authority-from-work-approval\", \"title\": \"Authority from work approval\", \"anchor\": \"authority-from-work-approval\", \"start_line\": 134, \"end_line\": 163, \"content\": \"## Authority from work approval\\n\\nWork-order approval authorizes execution of its approved scope: start,\\nimplementation, local edits and commits, required checks, evidence capture,\\ncompletion recording, and required verification-record preparation. A Human\\nand an Agent MUST use the same local approval, scope, and evidence checks.\\nContinue these operations without asking for the same permission again while\\ntheir scope and conditions still match. Record execution under the actual\\nexecutor's identity.\\n\\nApproval leaves the work order `approved` until its start transition is\\napplied. Selecting and scheduling work remain explicit. New approvals need\\nno separate execution delegation table or second start approval. A preliminary\\nmerge and live CI are not local execution requirements; CI still applies at\\nintegration and publication.\\n\\nThe grant does not include changed scope, acceptance of the result, release,\\nmerge, publication, or other external action. Reuse an existing explicit\\nauthorization for such an action only when its exact target and conditions\\nstill match.\\n\\nHistorical approvals keep their original meaning. Earlier explicit execution\\ngrants remain usable under the same scope checks. An older approval without\\nsuch a grant needs an authorized amendment for the remaining execution.\\nCurrent metadata or an actor label MUST NOT manufacture that historical grant.\\nIf the installed release cannot record the required authorization, stop the\\nremaining execution and report the missing historical grant and the lack of\\na supported amendment operation. Do not insert invented grant fields or\\nrewrite the old approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"a0ce00fccb8c1c8833455c14fe3ec75eaf01c597f0a1f46a24f7962ab1958f8c\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"f094e7bc796ba1002c3fb2848adaccce6c4887e3d92503911bcafa4504a644fe\", \"bytes\": 11979, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe evaluator selects work start, implementation or completion under matching authority.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"b7fc3711b437c73d3cc5cce46c0a8bb7f1bc3c8c98ba650b4b046950d69a7652\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead the selected work order and governing artifacts returned by the evaluator. Before using its execution grant, read [AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval](AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval). For a scope finding, read [RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked](RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"5581ca4e192b2a3847952cdb19582916ed96242a14941b1e5a8a610be996e1dd\"}, {\"heading\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"title\": \"Start the selected work\", \"anchor\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"start_line\": 70, \"end_line\": 104, \"content\": \"### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2275126fa8b418eccbaacb1972d0140709337470ba8d190b6566fd16854328d1\"}, {\"heading\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"title\": \"Retain the implementation evidence\", \"anchor\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"start_line\": 155, \"end_line\": 210, \"content\": \"### Retain the implementation evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented changes, verification contracts (VER), required\\nrepository checks, and work-order evidence locations.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** Actual check results, review findings, their\\n  resolution, and Git-derived handoff evidence at authorized evidence paths.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Evidence references may be added where the work order\\n  (WO) requires them. No lifecycle state changes here.\\n- **Transient working material:** A summary of remaining evidence gaps.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run every required check using its specified inputs and environment.\\n2. Retain the command arguments, working directory, runtime identity, exit\\n   status, output, and relevant file paths or digests.\\n3. Preserve failed results alongside later corrections and successful reruns.\\n4. Retain the material diff-review findings and their resolution.\\n5. Confirm that all required evidence files exist at the locations named by\\n   the work order (WO) or verification contract (VER).\\n6. Run review preflight.\\n7. Run the Git-derived handoff check with the trusted `BASE` from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n8. Inspect its written evidence and reported findings. Correct in-scope gaps\\n   and rerun affected checks.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase review --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nFor ordinary files listed in the work order\\u2019s `evidence_paths`, confirm that\\neach file exists inside the repository and contains the required evidence.\\nThese files do not need a machine header. Use the supported evidence command\\nfor generated packets; do not edit their evaluator-owned binding fields.\\n\\nThe Git-derived handoff command writes retained evidence. Confirm its output\\npaths are covered by the approved preparation scope. If the evaluator calls\\nfor creating or rebinding the handoff evidence packet, use:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl evidence REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --json\\n```\\n\\nThis command also writes a file. It does not run tests or make an unsupported\\nclaim true. Rerun the required handoff check after correcting the evidence gap.\\n\\n**Completion:** Required implementation checks pass and the actual evidence\\nis retained, or the exact missing or failed checks remain visible.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) requires passing handoff results. Verification later\\nassesses these retained files; they are not disposable planning notes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"4cccfe03ac5fe219da997051bb91053576f18e67e834f0036f93e9856e8f12a2\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/RESULTS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\RESULTS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"090e0172a78ebedfe4cdbe98e74c171e50215766a4ebbd671844a89af3f72e6e\", \"bytes\": 5242, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nReport a governed result, handle a refusal or recover an uncertain operation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"680ff724251b96e21b52d9104a0e6f2806e6f4269237b555518e7d72d20463a7\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead only the actual selected result and evidence needed to establish its effects. Before eligible English reporting, read [COMMUNICATION.md#communication](COMMUNICATION.md#communication) if it is no longer in context.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"22e8f880f3e2c5192c3f6abf5f63c7542b51647503ba0fb877c5acb70c7a5837\"}, {\"heading\": \"report-a-lifecycle-result\", \"title\": \"Report a lifecycle result\", \"anchor\": \"report-a-lifecycle-result\", \"start_line\": 13, \"end_line\": 46, \"content\": \"### Report a lifecycle result\\n\\nAfter a lifecycle stage completes or reaches a stop condition, obtain the\\nselected evaluator's schema-2 result. The result controls the reported scope,\\neffects, blockers, final state, required decision, alternatives, and next step.\\nThe human or agent MUST NOT recompute those fields.\\n\\nFor a human-readable report, lead with the outcome or required decision. The\\nwording and headings may vary, and empty fields may be omitted. The report\\nMUST preserve:\\n\\n1. The actual artifact IDs and whether the selected operation completed or\\n   was blocked.\\n2. The observed effects, separately from expected effects that did not occur.\\n3. Every exact blocker and every material non-effect. In particular, do not\\n   imply approval, a transition, verification, release, a Git action,\\n   publication, deployment, or operation that did not occur.\\n4. The final lifecycle state and the accountable human or agent. When a\\n   decision is required, name the exact decision and authority needed.\\n5. Exactly one current typed procedure step from the evaluator.\\n6. The command's argument values and boundaries, or the operative meaning of\\n   the suggested response.\\n\\nOnly complete alternatives declared by the workflow may be shown. Present them\\nseparately from the primary next step. Do not invent an effect, authority,\\nblocker, decision, or alternative. Do not add unrelated repository findings\\nto the selected result. Do not replace the returned next step with an\\nopen-ended question about what to do next.\\n\\nAutomation MUST read the structured JSON result. New results declare\\n`digest_format = \\\"machine-fields-v1\\\"`. This digest binds machine fields such\\nas candidate identity, scope, state, gates, and command arguments. Explanatory\\nwording is excluded. Older retained results keep their original digest format.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"d12f5fc7e6190762622666754fde9b70e658249ceb41f2fdbf48e5e0edbd0f8f\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"delegation-and-separation\", \"title\": \"Delegation and separation\", \"anchor\": \"delegation-and-separation\", \"start_line\": 111, \"end_line\": 133, \"content\": \"## Delegation and separation\\n\\nA human decision right may be delegated only to another human who is permitted\\nto hold it. The delegation MUST name the right, its scope, the granting human,\\nand the receiving human. It MUST be recorded before the delegated decision.\\nThe receiving human MUST decide under their own identity.\\n\\nAn instruction for an agent to apply a recorded human decision delegates the\\nexecution of that command. It does not delegate the decision itself. The\\nagent MUST NOT supply a new answer, broaden the targets, or claim the human\\nmade a decision that was not given.\\n\\nRepository policy MAY require different people for conflicting decisions on\\nthe same candidate. When it does, the same human MUST NOT hold both decisions\\nfor that candidate. A human MUST NOT approve their own architecture decision\\nassessment unless they explicitly hold approval authority for that artifact\\nand the separation rules permit it. Drafting an assessment grants no such\\nright.\\n\\nWhen the accountable human or required authority is absent or ambiguous, stop\\nthe affected decision. State the exact right and artifact needing an owner.\\nDo not substitute an available human or agent merely to fill the field.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"f07389fbbe529ad544d8b7d7f18894bfa45f0ec69996c93ed8372416b55ddf91\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-321\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"f094e7bc796ba1002c3fb2848adaccce6c4887e3d92503911bcafa4504a644fe\", \"bytes\": 11979, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe evaluator selects work start, implementation or completion under matching authority.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"b7fc3711b437c73d3cc5cce46c0a8bb7f1bc3c8c98ba650b4b046950d69a7652\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead the selected work order and governing artifacts returned by the evaluator. Before using its execution grant, read [AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval](AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval). For a scope finding, read [RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked](RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"5581ca4e192b2a3847952cdb19582916ed96242a14941b1e5a8a610be996e1dd\"}, {\"heading\": \"establish-the-execution-context\", \"title\": \"Establish the execution context\", \"anchor\": \"establish-the-execution-context\", \"start_line\": 32, \"end_line\": 69, \"content\": \"### Establish the execution context\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected work order (WO) ID and its recorded approval.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** None changed.\\n- **Transient working material:** The current execution scope, reading list,\\n  applicable procedure, and trusted Git base for the complete change set.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Inspect the selected work order (WO).\\n2. Read the formal artifacts in `context.reading_manifest`. For instructions,\\n   read the selected procedure's entry conditions, current step, and prerequisites\\n   whose conditions apply. Reuse current required material still in context;\\n   reread it when changed or lost. After compaction, obtain a fresh `check`\\n   result before continuing. This does not remove any required formal input.\\n3. Read the approved behavior, permitted paths, decision limits, stop\\n   conditions, verification contracts (VER), and required repository checks.\\n4. Identify the complete change baseline required by the selected procedure.\\n   Retain its full commit ID as `BASE`. Do not choose a newer base merely to\\n   exclude earlier changes from the checks.\\n5. Compare the planned work with the recorded approval. Return changed scope\\n   to definition and authorization before executing it.\\n\\n**Harness command:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The executor has an explicit approved scope and the inputs\\nrequired by the selected procedure. Missing baseline or scope information\\nis reported before dependent work.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Start the selected work](EXECUTE_WORK.md#start-the-selected-work), [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope), [Retain the implementation evidence](EXECUTE_WORK.md#retain-the-implementation-evidence), [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) use this scope, baseline, and verification plan.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6dc0a4c1efd03f2db0baf6eb2863efdf6b55d5578989d42864ac9398d9d31aea\"}]}]}",

## Event 666: assistant text

The valid handoff is blocked, so the diagnostic is incomplete. The out-of-scope refusal also came from a different layer than intended, and I did not demonstrate the stale preview. Everything before the handoff worked. All records here are test data in the hosted rehearsal; Git remains authoritative, and nothing in this run verifies real work, completes NQ-01 to NQ-05, or authorizes a release.

**What worked**
- **Records:** I imported the fixture and wrote six new records under `docs/engineering/lifecycle-pilot/`: `INT-P3-001`, `CAP-P3-001`, `REQ-P3-001`, `SPEC-P3-001`, `VER-P3-001` and `WO-P3-001`. Validation found 0 errors.
- **Approvals and start:** the five definitions and the work order were approved by the synthetic `test-owner`, and all gates passed. `test-executor` started the work order after start preflight reported ready. No source file was changed, and the imported `-900` records were not touched.
- **Cypher reads:** two reads through the MCP Cypher tool returned complete results. The first confirmed the work order's links to `REQ-P3-001`, `SPEC-P3-001` and `VER-P3-001`. The second confirmed the work order is still `in_progress` after the refusals.
- **Test run:** the fixture assertion passed (exit 0, `exact greeting passed`). It differs from the check written in `VER-P3-001`: it ran on the local Windows Python, skipped the check that the result is a string, and was not tied to a commit. These gaps are recorded in the evidence file.

**What failed**
1. **Out-of-scope handoff (two attempts):** both were refused by the service before the evaluator ran, with `HAG_REMOTE_PROTECTED_FIELD` ("Handoff inputs cannot replace formal or instruction files"). This is a protected-file rule, not the evaluator's scope check I set out to show. The service didn't say which file triggered it, but the in-scope file was accepted when sent alone, so the out-of-scope paths caused it.
2. **Valid handoff:** the evaluator refused it. One check failed: `WEX201: changed path is outside execution scope: docs/engineering/lifecycle-pilot/capabilities/CAP-P3-001.md`. The cause is my work order's scope. The comparison base is the Git state before the definitions were approved, so those approvals count as changes. The scope only allows the evidence file, so the five definition files fall outside it. The procedure doesn't allow picking a later base or widening an approved scope without a decision. I stopped there.
3. **Stale preview:** not shown. It needs a preview that passes, and none was available once the handoff was blocked.

None of the refusals changed anything: the project version is still 17 and the work order is still `in_progress`.

**Decision needed:** `test-owner` must decide, under `DR-REMEDIATION-SCOPE`, whether to correct `WO-P3-001`'s scope to include the five definition files. If approved, I would preview the handoff again and then attempt the stale-preview demonstration.

Files are in `work/`:
- native-report.md
- native-observations.json
- r*/l*/x* records (requests and results)
- z01-observations (call trace: 109 native calls, 8 errors, all explained in the report)
